# Claims register

Every claim sentence of the paper (the abstract, the introduction, the six hypotheses as planned and as read back in the appendix The Hypotheses Read Back, a section of the body until 21 November 2026, every number-bearing sentence of Section 6, the ablations, the discussion's RQ1 to RQ3, the limitations and the conclusion), each with the report file and key that holds its evidence under `paper/data/run9-visible-exposure` (run 9: exposure fixed on visible rows and a Student-t base rate as the main run, commit b2e3713). The register was rebuilt against the rewritten paper on 14 November 2026, row by row: a row whose claim the paper still makes carries the sentence as now printed and its run 9 key; a row whose claim left the paper keeps its id with status removed and a note; rows C601 and above were added for claims the rewritten sections make. Ids are stable and are never reused.

Conventions. The sentence column quotes the clause that carries the claim, with LaTeX removed. The report path is relative to the repository root. The key path uses `tables.<name>[<col>=<value>,...].<field>` for a row of a table, plain dots for scalars, and `[" "]` around a dictionary key that itself contains a dot (the gates file). The printed value is exactly what the paper prints: "minus x" and "plus x" carry the sign, "N percent" and "N points" are the key's value times 100, a count keeps its thousands separator, and a verdict string or a boolean is quoted as the file holds it; a key path may start with `neg:` (the paper prints the difference the other way round: C4, C101, C324) or `pct:` (the paper prints percentage points of a fraction: C14). The interval kind is adjusted when the sentence rests on a Bonferroni-adjusted bound, plain when it quotes a 90 percent by-test interval, and none otherwise. A design or protocol claim has no report, key or value.

| C18 | abstract | Tools. Forecasts come from a base rate with a heavy-tailed density, a prior fitted to past tests, that prior moved toward similar past tests, and three learned models, one of them a text classifier. Release. The protocol comes with Moot Court, an MIT-licensed Python package that regenerates every results table and figure from its reports and accepts a company private aggregates in the same format (that this path has run only on a bundled sample ledger, and that the package has not been deployed in production, left the abstract and are in contribution 4, C25); paper/dist/metadata.md carries a condensed abstract for arXiv with the six exploratory findings and the confirmatory one, and leaves Tools to the PDF | none | none | none | none | supported |

| C156 | conclusion | On one publisher archive, a base rate with a heavy-tailed density, fitted to concluded experiments, predicts how the next result will be distributed nearly as well as a prior fitted to the same experiments; the prior pooled lead is small; its interval lies above zero with the seven scored quarters held fixed, and with the quarters as the units it contains zero, so the lead is not shown; seven values are few, and that interval treats them as exchangeable; without the quarter of July 2014 the pooled interval contains zero as well (C395), and with January 2014 left out the interval with the quarters as the units lies above zero, so the reading turns on single quarters either way (added in the fifth revision, C1197); both forecasters beat a reference that uses no past effect and assumes no test has one, a floor, and nearly all of the gain over it is already in the base rate (C608); more history improved the forecast at each of the seven scored origins (C607); the prior skill on which test will be significant is under one percent of the base rate score, and it is a better level per cell and quarter, because within a cell and quarter the prior gives every test the same probability (no number printed but the counts; the numbers are C602, C603, C604, C6, C174 and C290; the first specification, where the lead looked far larger, is C4; the clause on four choices and the 0.0069 left the conclusion with run 9) (21 November 2026, the conclusion shortened at the user's decision: it now reads: a base rate with a heavy-tailed density predicts how the next result will be distributed nearly as well as a fitted prior, whose small lead is not shown with the seven scored quarters as the units, with a reference to Section 6.2 for the limits of that interval; more history improved the forecast at each scored origin; the prior's skill on which test will be significant is small and fragile, a better level and not a ranking of tests; the clauses on the pooled interval with the quarters held fixed, on the two single quarters and on the reference that uses no past effect left the conclusion and stay in Section 6.2, C604, C394, C724 and C504; | C277 | 4 | Pooling: two fits are each shrunk toward the global fit g, with weight n/(n + 20), where n is the count of visible rows behind the fit: the cell's own fit, and the fit of the rows of all cells whose assigned end falls in one calendar quarter, the period; in that period the cell's parameter is K + (P minus g)/2, applied to the mean and to the log of the standard deviation, and the factor of one half is fixed, not estimated; the period fits use the global nu; a cell whose weight is at least one half keeps its own nu; two rules state which quarter's level a forecast takes: a quarter contributes nothing if it has fewer than 30 visible rows or ends after the fit's data, and the quarter being forecast has no results yet, so every forecast takes the level of the most recent complete quarter that has at least 30 visible rows (before the fourth revision the list held three rules; the third, on the level taken by a fit inside a quarter, left the method section) (src/mootcourt/predictors/p1.py cell_params, last_period, fit; the key is the count of scored tests those fits forecast) | paper/data/run9-visible-exposure/backtest-upworthy-20261106-2dc461f52ea4.json | header.tests_scored | 2,949 | none | supported |

Rows after the rebuild of 14 November 2026, the revisions that followed reviews that day and the confirmatory run of 15 November 2026: 1069 in all, 116 removed, 782 supported, 171 weakened. Rows C1161 to C1186 were added for claims the fourth revision makes (commit 53bc0ba), rows C1187 to C1216 for claims the fifth revision makes (commit c99ebfc), rows C1217 to C1223 for claims the sixth revision makes, and rows C1224 and above for the skill against a smoothed base rate, a report added after that revision (the seventh revision, at code state b031ed8). Status weakened marks a row where the paper now claims less than the row once recorded. Rows C1273 to C1276 were added in the eighth review round of the same day, rows C1277 to C1281 for the wording edits that followed the ninth round, rows C1282 and C1283 for the deferred edits of the tenth, row C1284 for the eleventh, and rows C1285 to C1287 for the review before the merge. Rows C1263 to C1272 were added in the seventh review round of 15 November 2026 (the precedent rung by cell, the readings behind the first two verdicts and the second confirmatory table). Rows C1233 to C1262 were added for the registered confirmatory analysis of 15 November 2026 (Section sec:confirmatory, run folder paper/data/run10-confirmatory); their report paths name that folder from the repository root, so the audit resolves them whatever run folder it is given, when it is run from the root. That section was inserted between the results and the ablations, so a section number of 7 or more in the section column of an earlier row is one less than the number now printed. The history of the register before run 9 is in the git history of this file. Rows C1290 to C1327 were added on 20 November 2026 for the simulated readers (Section sec:readers and its appendix; run folders paper/data/run11-simulated-readers and paper/data/run12-simulated-readers-api, named from the repository root). Rows C1328 and C1329 were added on 21 November 2026 for the closest earlier work on weights over personas, once its article had been read. That section was inserted between the hypotheses and the discussion, so the printed numbers of the discussion, the limitations, the reproducibility section and the conclusion are one more than an earlier row's section column says. Rows C1330 to C1367 were added on 21 November 2026 after a review by four fresh readers, for the claims the revision that followed it makes. In the same revision rows C25, C39, C40, C163, C268, C288, C368, C369, C374, C1019, C1146, C1161, C1180, C1181, C1187, C1282, C1284, C1287, C1289, C1296, C1298, C1299, C1302, C1306, C1309, C1312, C1315, C1325, C1327 and C1329 were revised to the words the paper now prints; of these, rows C39, C268, C368, C374, C1289, C1299 and C1329 changed from supported to weakened. The counts above include these rows. On 21 November 2026, at the author's decision after two reviews, four appendix parts left the paper and moved to docs/paper-supplement.md: wall time, latency and scale; the tooling landscape; the weekly replay detail; and the check of the mechanics on a second archive, of which a short summary stays in the appendix. Their 30 rows are now removed: C88, C89, C90, C117, C170, C215, C216, C257, C291, C315, C371, C376, C478, C479, C480, C481, C482, C872, C891 to C896 and C911 to C916. Rows C214, C217, C218, C870, C871 and C897 stay with that summary. On the same day the section that reads the hypotheses back and the subsection on abstention, the card and the loop became appendix sections in both builds; the 84 rows of the first and rows C424, C425 and C428 of the second keep their status and name the appendix in their section column. The counts above include these changes. On 21 November 2026 rows were also marked removed for sentences cut from the conclusion, the limitations and the results at the user's decision to shorten the paper: C4, C395, C608, C1197 and C1275 for the conclusion and C1087 for the limitations, each naming the row that still carries its claim; rows whose quoted words changed in those cuts, in the introduction and the confirmatory section too, carry a note with that date and keep their status. The counts above include these rows.
